=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a binary string s of length n, where:

'1' represents an active section.
'0' represents an inactive section.

You can perform at most one trade to maximize the number of active sections in s. In a trade, you:

Convert a contiguous block of '1's that is surrounded by '0's to all '0's.
Afterward, convert a contiguous block of '0's that is surrounded by '1's to all '1's.

Return the maximum number of active sections in s after making the optimal trade.
Note: Treat s as if it is augmented with a '1' at both ends, forming t = '1' + s + '1'. The augmented '1's do not contribute to the final count.
 
Example 1:

Input: s = "01"
Output: 1
Explanation:
Because there is no block of '1's surrounded by '0's, no valid trade is possible. The maximum number of active sections is 1.

Example 2:

Input: s = "0100"
Output: 4
Explanation:

String "0100" → Augmented to "101001".
Choose "0100", convert "101001" → "100001" → "111111".
The final string without augmentation is "1111". The maximum number of active sections is 4.


Example 3:

Input: s = "1000100"
Output: 7
Explanation:

String "1000100" → Augmented to "110001001".
Choose "000100", convert "110001001" → "110000001" → "111111111".
The final string without augmentation is "1111111". The maximum number of active sections is 7.


Example 4:

Input: s = "01010"
Output: 4
Explanation:

String "01010" → Augmented to "1010101".
Choose "010", convert "1010101" → "1000101" → "1111101".
The final string without augmentation is "11110". The maximum number of active sections is 4.


 
Constraints:

1 <= n == s.length <= 10^5
s[i] is either '0' or '1'

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxActiveSectionsAfterTrade(self, s: str) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's design algorithm:

class Solution:
    def maxActiveSectionsAfterTrade(self, s: str) -> int:
        ones = s.count('1')
        t = '1' + s + '1'
        # build run lengths of t
        runs = []
        i=0
        N=len(t)
        while i<N:
            j=i
            while j<N and t[j]==t[i]:
                j+=1
            runs.append(j-i)
            i=j
        # runs starts and ends with '1' because t[0]=t[-1]='1'
        # internal one runs are at even indices 2,4,...,len(runs)-3? Need handle.
        max_gain=0
        # Since runs[0] is ones, runs[1] zeros, runs[2] ones, ...
        # For idx from 2 to len(runs)-2? Let's derive.
        # K = len(runs). One-run indices: 0,2,4,...,K-1 (K odd). Internal one-run indices: 2,4,...,K-3.
        # If K >=5, there is at least one internal one-run.
        # For idx in range(2, K-1, 2)? Wait K-1 is last one-run boundary, not internal. range(2, K-1, 2) excludes K-1. But if K=5, range(2,4,2) -> idx2, K-1=4, ok. If K=7, range(2,6,2) -> 2,4; K-3=4, ok. If K=3, range(2,2,2) empty. So range(2, len(runs)-1, 2) works? For K=5, len-1=4, range(2,4,2) includes 2. For K=7, range(2,6,2) includes 2,4. Good. But need ensure idx+1 < len(runs), yes for idx <= K-3? Wait range(2,K-1,2) includes K-3 if K-3 even? K odd => K-3 even. It stops before K-1. Good. But if K=5, K-3=2. Good. If K=1, range(2,0,2) empty.
        for idx in range(2, len(runs)-1, 2):
            gain = runs[idx-1] + runs[idx+1]
            max_gain = max(max_gain, gain)
        return ones + max_gain

But wait range(2, len(runs)-1, 2) with len(runs)-1 as stop. For K=5, stop=4, includes 2. For K=7, stop=6, includes 2,4. It excludes stop 6 (boundary one-run). Good. But what about K=3, stop=2, range(2,2,2) empty. Good. What about K=1, stop=0, empty. So OK.

Yes, in t, zero runs alternate with one runs; any consecutive zero runs in the list are separated by an internal one run. There are k zero runs, k-1 internal one runs? This might be easier: count zero runs in t and find max sum of adjacent zero run lengths. But need ensure zero runs list length >=2. Wait zero runs in t are surrounded by ones by construction, yes. The second conversion can convert any zero run in t after first conversion. The zero run chosen for max gain is the merged one, which is not necessarily an original zero run. But gain equals sum of two adjacent original zero runs. So max adjacent zero run sum.

Let's consider if the first one run chosen could be the one between zero runs z_i and z_{i+1}. The merged zero run length = z_i + r_i + z_{i+1}; converting it to ones yields gain z_i+z_{i+1}. Good.

Now, let's think if there is a possibility to choose first one run and then second zero run that is the merged zero run but maybe the merged zero run is not "surrounded by '1's" because one of the adjacent one runs is the augmented boundary? If chosen one run is last internal, right neighbor boundary right ones. So valid. Good.

Let's think about "at most one trade" maybe if no valid first block, but there is a valid second block? No, trade requires first. If no internal one run, no trade. If internal one run exists, trade possible. We always do it because gain positive. But what if max_gain = 0? Not possible if internal one run exists because zero run lengths positive. So answer = ones + max_gain. If no internal, max_gain remains 0, answer ones. So no need max(0, max_gain). But if using max_gain initialized 0, OK.

Now, let's consider if there are multiple internal one runs and choosing one with max adjacent zero sum is always optimal. We proved. But let's test a weird case where choosing first one run with small gain and second zero run elsewhere gives larger than max adjacent sum? Suppose zero runs lengths [100,1,100], internal one runs between z1-z2 and z2-z3. Max adjacent sum = max(101,101)=101. If choose O1 (between 100 and1) and second zero run z3=100, net =100 - |O1|. In general any zero run length <= max adjacent sum because it is included in one of the adjacent sums with another positive zero run. Since zero run lengths positive, z_j < z_{j-1}+z_j or z_j+z_{j+1} (except boundaries? For boundary zero run z1, adjacent sum z1+z2 > z1; for last, z_{k-1}+z_k > z_k). Thus net choosing second elsewhere <= z_j <= max adjacent sum. So max adjacent sum is global optimum. Good.

Let's think if first one run length r_i could be zero? No, internal one run length >=1. If choosing second zero run elsewhere, net z_j - r_i < z_j, so strictly less than some adjacent sum. So no issue.

Now, let's consider if the second conversion could choose a zero run that is not maximal but "contiguous block of '0's surrounded by '1's" maybe a sub-block of a zero run with ones on both sides? If you choose a sub-block inside a larger zero run, its immediate neighbors are zeros, not ones, so invalid. If you choose a zero block that spans across a one run? No, contiguous zeros only. So maximal.

Implementation:
class Solution:
    def maxActiveSectionsAfterTrade(self, s: str) -> int:
        ones = s.count('1')
        t = '1' + s + '1'
        runs = []
        i = 0
        n = len(t)
        while i < n:
            j = i + 1
            while j < n and t[j] == t[i]:
                j += 1
            runs.append(j - i)
            i = j
        
        best = 0
        # runs[0] and runs[-1] are boundary one-runs.
        # Internal one-runs are at indices 2, 4, ..., len(runs)-2? Wait check.
        # Let's re-evaluate range.
        # If runs = [1,1,1,2,1], len=5. Internal one-run index 2. len(runs)-2 =3. range(2, len(runs)-1, 2) -> range(2,4,2) includes2. Good.
        # If runs = [1,1,1,1,1,1,1], len=7. Internal one-run indices 2,4. len(runs)-2=5. range(2,6,2) includes2,4. Good.
        # If runs = [2,3,1] len=3. range(2,2,2) empty. Good.
        # If runs = [5] len=1. range(2,0,2) empty.
        for idx in range(2, len(runs) - 1, 2):
            best = max(best, runs[idx - 1] + runs[idx + 1])
        return ones + best

Need ensure idx+1 exists for idx = len(runs)-2? Wait range stop len(runs)-1, so last idx is len(runs)-3 if len(runs)-1 is even? We don't access runs[idx+1] for boundary last one-run index len-1 because stop excludes. Good.

Let's ensure code style:
```python
class Solution:
    def maxActiveSectionsAfterTrade(self, s: str) -> int:
        ones = s.count('1')
        t = '1' + s + '1'
        runs = []
        i = 0
        while i < len(t):
            j = i + 1
            while j < len(t) and t[j] == t[i]:
                j += 1
            runs.append(j - i)
            i = j

        best_gain = 0
        for idx in range(2, len(runs) - 1, 2):
            best_gain = max(best_gain, runs[idx - 1] + runs[idx + 1])

        return ones + best_gain
```

ones = s.count('1')
zero_groups = []
i=0
while i<n:
    if s[i]=='0':
        j=i
        while j<n and s[j]=='0':
            j+=1
        zero_groups.append(j-i)
        i=j
    else:
        i+=1
if len(zero_groups) < 2:
    return ones
best = max(zero_groups[i] + zero_groups[i+1] for i in range(len(zero_groups)-1))
return ones + best

ones = s.count('1')
zeros = []
i=0
while i < len(s):
    if s[i] == '0':
        j=i
        while j < len(s) and s[j]=='0':
            j+=1
        zeros.append(j-i)
        i=j
    else:
        i+=1
if len(zeros) < 2:
    return ones
best = 0
for i in range(len(zeros)-1):
    best = max(best, zeros[i]+zeros[i+1])
return ones+best

Let's maybe think if there is any issue with "contiguous block of '0's that is surrounded by '1's" after first conversion: If we choose first one run between zero groups A and B, the merged zero block includes A + one run + B. Let's maybe think if we should avoid `s.count('1')` because it scans twice? O(n) fine. Could count ones during scan to be more efficient:
ones = 0
zeros = []
i=0
while i<n:
    if s[i]=='0':
        j=i
        while j<n and s[j]=='0':
            j+=1
        zeros.append(j-i)
        i=j
    else:
        ones += 1

```
class Solution:
    def maxActiveSectionsAfterTrade(self, s: str) -> int:
        ones = s.count('1')
        prev_zero = -1
        best = 0
        i = 0
        n = len(s)
        while i < n:
            if s[i] == '0':
                j = i
                while j < n and s[j] == '0':
                    j += 1
                cur = j - i
                if prev_zero != -1:
                    best = max(best, prev_zero + cur)
                prev_zero = cur
                i = j
            else:
                i += 1
        return ones + best
```

s="01": cur1 prev=-1 prev1; return1.
s="1": no zeros, best0 return1.
s="0": cur1 prev=-1 prev1; return0.

Now, let's decide final code. I'll use zero groups list version. Let's write final code:

```python
class Solution:
    def maxActiveSectionsAfterTrade(self, s: str) -> int:
        ones = s.count('1')
        zero_runs = []
        n = len(s)
        i = 0
        while i < n:
            if s[i] == '0':
                j = i
                while j < n and s[j] == '0':
                    j += 1
                zero_runs.append(j - i)
                i = j
            else:
                i += 1

        if len(zero_runs) < 2:
            return ones

        best_gain = 0
        for i in range(len(zero_runs) - 1):
            best_gain = max(best_gain, zero_runs[i] + zero_runs[i + 1])

        return ones + best_gain
```

Let's verify with example2: zero_runs [1,2], best3, ones1, return4. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "zero_runs" if
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
was cut off due to length. Need final code only. Ensure not too verbose.</think>```python
class Solution:
    def maxActiveSectionsAfterTrade(self, s: str) -> int:
        ones = s.count('1')
        zero_runs = []
        n = len(s)
        i = 0

        while i < n:
            if s[i] == '0':
                j = i
                while j < n and s[j] == '0':
                    j += 1
                zero_runs.append(j - i)
                i = j
            else:
                i += 1

        if len(zero_runs) < 2:
            return ones

        best_gain = 0
        for i in range(len(zero_runs) - 1):
            best_gain = max(best_gain, zero_runs[i] + zero_runs[i + 1])

        return ones + best_gain
```
